In separate talks, AMD’s chief Zen architect Mike Clark and corporate VP Madhu Rangarajan said that the company didn’t predict the agentic wave when Zen 6 development started.

We just wrapped up testing AMD's Venice CPUs, which you can read more about in our Zen 6 benchmarking article.

CPU development cycles are measured in years, and given that the agentic frenzy around CPUs hit around March of this year, it’s unreasonable to expect that anyone has developed silicon specifically for agentic AI, much less a general-purpose data center CPU. And still, Nvidia claims its Vera CPU was “purpose-built” for agentic AI, while Arm says its AGI CPU was “designed for the demands of agentic AI.”

“We think that the EPYC 9006 CPU is the best CPU for the agentic era, and spoiler alert, we didn’t magically know four years ago that agentic was going to be a thing and purpose-built something for agentic,” said Rangarajan in a session with media. “The reality is an agentic workflow is a diverse set of tasks… and in order to execute those tasks well, you need compute that is able to handle a diverse set of workloads with a full portfolio.”

Introducing the Zen 6 microarchitecture, Mike Clark agreed with the sentiment: “I’m not going to mislead and act like I knew agentic computing was coming, or we knew it, but we did strongly believe that the CPU would always have a place in the software ecosystem of AI… And as agentic [has] blown up, we [are] able to now deploy that for agentic compute workloads.”

As AMD rolls out its next-generation microarchitecture with Venice CPUs, it’s targeting the messaging from Arm and Nvidia, which have posed the most direct threat to AMD’s continued rise in the data center on the back of agentic workloads. And indeed, there are performance vectors where Nvidia’s Vera looks strong for agentic workloads, as you can see in our Nvidia Vera testing. AMD’s argument, however, is that agentic AI calls for a broad range of CPUs just as general-purpose workloads do, pushing back on the single-SKU approach of Nvidia and Arm.

Our testing of two AMD Venice chips supports that argument, as well, with the dense EPYC 9996 with its 256 cores and 512 threads offering peak throughput performance, while the frequency-optimized EPYC 9686F goes toe-to-toe with Vera in the role of an AI host node. AMD is launching two additional ranges next year, with Venice-X targeting HPC with its 3D-stack cache, and Verano going directly after Vera as an AI host node with an LPDDR5X memory system.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Although the workload characteristics of agentic AI are still in the process of being defined, we have a better understanding of how those loops work now than we did at the beginning of the year. And like all complex topics, the answer to what is important in a CPU for agentic AI is the always-disappointing “it depends.”

As we break down in our early testing procedure for agentic AI, the workload characteristics and demand on the CPU/memory are defined by what the agent is doing, and in the data center, what several agents are doing at high concurrency when sandboxed. A coding agent will generally call more tools that are taxing on the CPU than a web browsing agent, for example, while an agent that queries a database may call for strong memory performance.

Of course, there are some vectors of performance that can’t be addressed with different SKUs (such as that strong memory performance). However, the potential diversity of agentic workloads certainly makes an argument for CPU variety. At a most basic level, an agentic loop allows the LLM to understand functions you’ve defined, and then pass arguments to those functions to execute them. We’re talking about a workload as broad as what you can define in a function in languages like Python and Rust.

AMD winds up Zen 6 cores for faster clock speeds

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Chips boast up to 256 cores, clocks up to 5.3 GHz, and IPC gains of 20-31 percent, just not all on the same SKU

After teasing its 256-core Epycs for more than a year, AMD is finally ready to talk about architectural changes to the Zen core that not only make it possible, but will eventually power its next generation of Ryzen processors in desktop and mobile PCs.

During a press event in Las Vegas last week, AMD spilled the beans on its latest Zen cores, which promise an average 27 percent uplift in instructions per clock (IPC) across a variety of AI and server workloads, along with boosted clocks ranging from 4.1 to 5.3 GHz. We expect to see even higher frequencies on consumer chips before long, but AMD isn’t quite ready to talk about Ryzen just yet.

CPU performance is a function of power, IPC, and clocks, and with gains across all three, the cores are shaping up to be one of the company’s biggest generational jumps ever.

As in past Epyc launches, Zen 6 isn’t one core. It comes in two flavors: a frequency-optimized variant (Zen 6) and a density-optimized version (Zen 6c).

This split dates back to the launch of AMD’s Bergamo Epycs in 2023, which packed 128 compact Zen 4c cores into just eight chiplets. The chip’s debut drew comparisons to Intel’s still relatively new Efficient (E-cores) for obvious reasons. But under the hood, AMD’s Zen 4c was nothing like what Intel was doing. The core itself was nearly identical to Zen 4; it simply traded clocks and a bit of cache per core for a more densely packed CPU die.

The only difference between running an application on Bergamo or Genoa was how quickly it executed. The same could not be said of Intel’s P and E cores, which used different ISAs.

This remained a defining characteristic and advantage of AMD’s compact core architecture through Zen 5, which allowed it to ship Turin parts with up to 192 cores without users having to worry about whether their app was running on a compact core or not.

With Zen 6c, at least as we understand it, this philosophy is largely unchanged. Both Zen 6 and Zen 6c share the same ISA, with clock being the main differentiator. There are other micro-architectural differences between the two cores designed to balance IPC and clock speeds more effectively.

Looking at the core’s block diagram, this isn’t immediately obvious. Zen 6 and Zen 5 share a very similar front and backend.

We'd be tempted to call the core iterative if it weren't for the 20 to 31 percent IPC uplift for cloud and AI workloads that AMD claims over Zen 5. This suggests that core for core, a 4.1 GHz Zen 6c-based Venice Epyc should deliver performance on par with a Zen 5-based Turin Epyc running at 5 GHz.

That's a pretty sizable jump, but just because AMD is seeing generational performance uplift in enterprise server and cloud workloads doesn't mean we'll see the same gains on the client side. Many will recall the roughly 5 percent performance gains gaming PC enthusiasts reported with the Ryzen 9000-series, leading to the rather unfortunate turn of phrase "Zen 5 percent."

That happened because game engines at the time simply weren't CPU-bottlenecked, and so faster cores could only raise the ceiling so much. Even so, AMD fellow and "Zen daddy" Mike Clark tells El Reg that PC buyers expect more than a 6 percent gain from Zen 6.

We're glossing over a bunch of low-level architectural optimizations here, but according to Clark, one of the biggest drivers of performance was to the front end. Zen 5 introduced a dual front end, which for simple workloads worked great, but lost a lot of steam when confronted with more dynamic loads.

"The Zen 6 team did a really good job for those larger footprint workloads that have a bipolar nature," Clark said. "It's not something that comes through. You're not going to see it in a block diagram very well. It's sort of subtle... but it's a big lever."

Another contributor was the move to a distributed scheduler in the out-of-order pipeline. One of the biggest performance drivers in modern CPU cores is that they don't execute instructions in the order they receive them. Instead, they reorder the instructions so as to keep the core's backend execution units fed with a constant stream of instructions.

This is where we see the biggest difference between Zen 6 and Zen 6c. To account for the slower clocks on the latter core, AMD gave the core's scheduler deeper queues, which Clark tells us helps with higher-latency workloads. Meanwhile, from what we understand, Zen 6's shallower queue enables it to clock higher on less power.

Finally, on the backend, AMD made several subtle changes which should have major implications for vector heavy workloads, including AI. One of the more significant was the ability to perform four AVX2 or two AVX-512 loads per cycle.

While this doesn't improve AVX-512 performance much, for applications that haven't been updated to use it, the core is now able to perform twice as many loads, which should improve throughput for legacy applications. This should come in handy, particularly on PCs, where Intel's outright lack of AVX-512 support has made AVX2 the lowest common denominator for a lot of software.

That's not to say AVX-512 didn't get some love this generation. For those running AI workloads on CPUs, Zen 6 now natively supports FP16 datatypes. Previously FP16 was supported, but required padding the data to FP32, which meant the lower precision didn't buy much in terms of performance. That's no longer the case in Zen 6.

And while we're on the topic of AI acceleration, Clark notes that Zen 6 doubled the VNNI-INT8 pipes from two to four, and should effectively double throughput for workloads that support it.

While not strictly part of the core, AMD's Zen 6 processors also address one of the biggest asymmetries associated with its frequency and density optimized cores.

Going back to Bergamo, opting for a density-optimized Epyc didn't just come at the expense of clocks, but cache too. Each Zen 4c or Zen 5c chiplet contained twice as many cores, but the same amount of L3 cache.

16 channels of DDR5, 5 GHz clock speeds, and support for CXL 3.1 memory god boxes? What's not to like?

With Zen 6 and Zen 6c, you may be giving up some clocks, but cache per core is now a constant 4 MB. As we discussed in a Venice deep dive back in September, each of the processor's eight CPU dies contain 12 or 32 cores and 48 and 128 MB respectively.

The benefit here is that users don't have to worry about different ISAs or cache hierarchies when optimizing their software stacks against AMD's Epyc processors. The same cannot be said of Intel's Xeon processors.

As we alluded to earlier, Chipzilla's performance and efficiency cores use different micro-architectures, ISAs, cache hierarchies, clocks, and even cluster their cores differently. This can pose real headaches if you have workloads that take advantage of fat vector registers like AVX-512, or accelerators like AMX, which will work just fine on Intel's P-cores but won't even run on their E-cores.

This, along with support for SMT (Hyperthreading), which Intel has dropped for now, could help the House of Zen vacuum up even more market share from its long-time rival. ®

Goldman, a lead bank on Anthropic’s IPO, sees $685B in revenue by 2030

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The forecast arrives as Anthropic starts selling its IPO to investors at a value of up to $1.4T.

Goldman Sachs analysts expect Anthropic’s revenue to reach about $685B by 2030, Bloomberg reported. Goldman is one of the banks leading Anthropic’s IPO, which began marketing on Tuesday at a value of up to $1.4T and is due to price on 18 November.

Goldman Sachs analysts expect Anthropic’s revenue to reach about $685B by 2030, nearly 150 times its sales last year, Bloomberg reported, citing a person familiar with the estimates. The Financial Times reported some of the figures earlier.

Goldman is one of the banks leading Anthropic’s IPO, which began marketing to investors on Tuesday. Anthropic is selling shares at $70 to $78 each to raise up to $64.4B, or $90.4B with a $26B private placement from Google, Nvidia and others, TheWrap reported.

That values Anthropic at up to $1.4T, counting stock options. The shares are due to price on 18 November and start trading on Nasdaq the next day under the ticker ANT.

Goldman’s analysts see $586B a year coming from business customers by 2030, and about $100B from everyone else. They expect Anthropic’s spending on computing to rise more than tenfold to nearly $400B a year. Anthropic would only start making positive free cash flow in 2030, they said.

The forecast runs well ahead of Anthropic’s own. Its internal forecasts reportedly pointed to $190B to $200B in revenue in 2028. Sales are growing fast, though, reaching nearly $17B in the third quarter alone.

Not everyone is convinced. Research firm New Constructs has called it the “most ridiculous IPO of 2026” and valued Anthropic at as little as $144B. Goldman and Anthropic declined to comment to Bloomberg.

This 4-Week Workout Program Uses Just 1 Dumbbell to Help You Get Stronger

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Build muscle and fry fat over this month-long workout plan.

YES, YOU CAN work every muscle in your body with one piece of gear. We love kettlebells, med balls, and resistance bands, but if we had to choose one piece of equipment, it would be a single dumbbell.

The game plan: Three times a week, you’ll grab the simplest tool in the gym and use it to build muscle and banish fat in a super-dense half-hour workout. It’s the most effective way to fry body fat, and it’s the perfect way to train for the holidays. It’s also exactly how Hollywood-based trainer Ben Bruno, C.F.S.C., guides time-strapped A-listers like Justin Timberlake and NBA star Klay Thompson. “They want quick, efficient workouts that hit everything,” Bruno says. “So we need to move fast.”

It doesn’t get more efficient than this light-speed, one-dumbbell workout.

Stand holding a dumbbell at your left shoulder, feet shoulder-width apart, knees slightly bent, glutes squeezed, and core tight. Keeping your chest level, straighten your arm to press the dumbbell overhead (b). Pause, then slowly lower the dumbbell back to your shoulder. That’s 1 rep; do 8 per arm.

Stand holding a dumbbell at your chest. Tighten your core and step back about three feet with your right leg. Keeping your chest up and the dumbbell high, bend your knees and lower your torso until your left thigh is parallel to the floor. Your right knee should be a few inches from the floor. Pause, then push off your right leg and stand back up. That’s 1 rep; do 10 per leg.

Eb says: Squeeze your glutes when you stand up. This will help you avoid lower-back pain when doing lunges.

Set a bench to a 45-degree angle and position yourself so your chest is against it; squeeze your abs and glutes. With your left arm out at shoulder height, hold a dumbbell in your right hand, arm hanging naturally. Squeeze your shoulder blades. This is the start. Keeping your shoulders and hips steady, row the dumbbell to your right hip. Pause, then return to the start.That’s 1 rep; do 10 per arm.

Eb says: This is more than a back move; your glutes and abs should be into it, too. Tighten them up so your torso doesn’t tip down to the side of the weight.

Lie on your back, holding a dumbbell in your left hand, arm straight, directly over your chest. Press your lower back into the floor and lift your legs slightly off the floor; lift your shoulder blades slightly, too. This is the start.Bend at the elbow and shoulder, lowering the dumbbell to your chest without losing torso position. Press back to the start. That’s 1 rep; do 8 per arm.

Eb says: Struggling with the position? Bend your knees to make it easier.

Lie on your back, knees bent and feet flat on the floor. Lift your right foot off the floor and tuck your right knee toward your chest. Tighten your core. Driving your left heel into the ground, push your lower back off the floor; squeeze your left glute as you do this. Pause, then return to the start. That’s 1 rep; do 10 per side.

Set up in pushup position, glutes and abs tight, right hand gripping a dumbbell. Squeeze your shoulder blades. Pull the dumbbell off the floor, rowing it toward your rib cage but keeping your hips and shoulders square. Pause. Return the dumbbell to the start. That’s 1 rep; do 8 per arm.

Eb says: Focus on flexing the oblique on the rowing side as hard as you canto keep your hips level.

Why SpaceX Could Be the Best Buy-and-Hold Stock of the Decade

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SpaceX is building multiple engines that could power the company's long-term growth.

Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX -0.66%) reported $14.6 billion in Q3 revenue, a 161% increase from the prior-year period. Net income reached $4.4 billion, compared with a $1.1 billion loss a year earlier. These are extraordinary numbers for a company that went public just five months ago. And while SpaceX is best known for launching rockets, its long-term growth prospects extend well beyond the space industry. Between its expanding Starlink satellite broadband network, reusable rockets, and growing artificial intelligence (AI) business, SpaceX could gain significant ground over the next decade.

SpaceX's connectivity business, which includes Starlink, generated $5.25 billion in third-quarter revenue, up 69% year over year, and operating income jumped 99% to approximately $2.37 billion.

Unlike other satellite network operators, SpaceX both manufactures and launches its own satellites. Its reusable rockets let the company deploy additional capacity without the need to rely on an external launch provider.

Starlink also serves businesses, airlines, ships, and government customers, giving it plenty of opportunities beyond providing residential internet service. Make no mistake: Its potential customer base remains substantial, particularly in regions where conventional broadband infrastructure is limited or unavailable. And demand is likely to remain strong for at least the next 10 to 12 years.

SpaceX is also becoming a major artificial intelligence company as it expands into AI infrastructure and software. Its "super intelligence" segment actually helped drive third-quarter revenue growth, adding $6.7 billion compared with the prior-year period.

The company also expanded its computing infrastructure to 1.7 gigawatts of installed capacity as of Sept. 30. That gives SpaceX exposure to two rapidly expanding markets: satellite communications and artificial intelligence.

Management is pursuing a plan to deploy space-based computing infrastructure, too, although that remains an ambitious and expensive undertaking with considerable technical uncertainty. Still, SpaceX already has something many emerging technology companies lack: substantial revenues and profits.

SpaceX went public in June at $135 per share, valuing the company at approximately $1.77 trillion. That's an enormous valuation, even for a business growing as quickly as SpaceX. The company is also spending aggressively to expand its infrastructure. In the third quarter, its capital expenditures reached approximately $19.6 billion. These investments could support future growth, but they also create financial risks if demand disappoints or major projects take longer than expected to complete.

Competition in AI, regulatory challenges, satellite replacement costs, and the expense of developing its Starship rocket platform could also put pressure on the company's profitability in the future. And don't assume SpaceX will continue to grow at the extraordinary rate it did in the third quarter for any great length of time.

That said, the combination of SpaceX's position in commercial spaceflight, its rapidly expanding satellite communications network, and its substantial AI business is one that no other company can match. If management can sustain strong revenue growth while improving cash generation, SpaceX could reward patient shareholders over the next decade.

Can you use WD-40 on your electronics?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The short answer: no. The long answer: there's a different WD-40 product you can use.

It can quiet your hinges, get rid of rust and even help break apart stuck LEGO bricks — WD-40 is the magic spray that can do just about anything. It has been around since 1953, originally developed for use in the aerospace industry (specifically, protecting the exterior of the Atlas Missile from rust and corrosion), and has become a staple in just about every home. However, when it comes to your electronics, WD-40 is one of the worst possible cleaners you could reach for.

Though the "WD" in the product's name stands for "water displacement," as a whole it contains a mix of petroleum-based lubricants and anti-corrosion ingredients alongside those designed to displace water; this is why it excels both at lubrication and cleaning. From its inception in the '50s, the company has grown exponentially, expanding both the ways customers can buy regular WD-40, like various bottle sizes and applicators, as well as adding new products to the line-up like other specialized lubricants and degreasers. Nowadays, there are some products the company makes that are more suitable for your electronics, but the original formula should stay far away from them.

There are multiple reasons why WD-40 and sensitive electronic parts don't mix. For one, it can be completely counterproductive when it comes to cleaning, as its unique chemical makeup leaves a residue behind that can actually attract more dust and dirt over time. This residue is great when used for something like greasing a hinge, but much less so on your devices. It might look clean at first, but that area is now going to be a grime magnet and just worsen the problem you were attempting to solve.

Even worse is that the residue left behind is both electrically conductive and flammable. This means that your device is essentially now a ticking bomb, not only for future grime but actual hazardous reactions, particularly if you've used it to do something like clean a USB port and it has reached key internal parts. Eventually, the residue's conductivity could cause a component to short out, or in extreme cases cause an electrical fire. As a whole, using WD-40 on your electronics is all drawbacks and no benefits.

If you're steadfastly dedicated to the WD-40 brand, they do offer an alternative you can safely utilize with your electronics. WD-40 Specialist Contact Cleaner is designed to leave no residue, be nonconductive and dry quickly, eliminating all the pitfalls associated with the original WD-40. It's not the only contact cleaner on the market, but for those who trust the brand and enjoy their straw-based applicator design, it's a solid choice.

However, a specialized product isn't necessarily a must-have purchase when it comes to keeping your electronics clean. An arsenal consisting solely of high-proof (90-99%) isopropyl alcohol for use on dirty components and a can of compressed air, which is great for things like cleaning laptop fans and keyboards, is generally enough to satisfy most needs, especially when paired with a good-quality microfiber cloth. For a deeper look at the best way to clean your devices, check out how to properly sanitize your tech.

Anthropic is targeting a valuation of up to $1.02 trillion, based on its proposed IPO price range of $70–$80 per share.

The company could raise $69.4 billion through its public offering and another $26 billion through a private placement.

Nvidia and Alphabet are each committing $10 billion, highlighting the financial exposure of major technology companies to Anthropic's growth.

Anthropic’s planned IPO is emerging as a major test for the AI industry, with the Claude developer targeting a valuation of approximately $1 trillion. Backed by Nvidia and other technology companies, the offering could raise more than $90 billion through public and private share sales. Its market debut comes as investors increasingly scrutinise the cost of developing artificial intelligence and the returns on infrastructure spending. Can Anthropic justify its ambitious valuation, and what could its listing mean for Nvidia and other AI stocks?

Anthropic has proposed selling 865 million shares at $70–$80 apiece, potentially raising up to $69.4 billion. Based on outstanding shares, the upper end of the range implies a valuation of approximately $1.02 trillion. Including options and other potential shares, the fully diluted valuation could reach around $1.4 trillion.

Anthropic is seeking to raise up to $69.3B in its IPO, below earlier expectations of $80B-$100B, Reuters reports.It plans to sell 866.7M new shares at $70-$80 each, with all proceeds going to the company.Trading could begin next week on Nasdaq under $ANT. pic.twitter.com/HoF0ENA2T6

The company is also arranging a separate $26 billion private placement involving Nvidia, Alphabet, Microsoft, AMD, Broadcom, Micron and TSMC. Google and Nvidia are each committing approximately $10 billion, making them the largest participants in the arrangement.

Anthropic plans to list on Nasdaq under the ticker symbol ANT. The offering’s final price and trading date remain subject to the IPO process.

Nvidia’s $10 billion commitment to Anthropic highlights its growing financial exposure to the AI ecosystem. The company already benefits from demand for GPUs used to train and operate advanced AI models. Anthropic’s expansion could support further demand for computing capacity, particularly as enterprise adoption of Claude increases.

However, the investment also raises questions about interconnected financing in the AI industry. Nvidia is investing in a company that needs substantial computing infrastructure, including its own products. Investors will therefore examine whether AI infrastructure spending is generating sustainable customer demand and revenue rather than depending heavily on capital flowing between industry participants.

Anthropic’s growth has been accompanied by substantial infrastructure commitments. Its latest filing disclosed approximately $17 billion in third-quarter revenue and $33.2 billion in revenue for the first nine months of 2026. However, the company also reported a nine-month net loss of $104 billion and computing and infrastructure commitments of approximately $900.9 billion.

The figures highlight the financial challenge facing advanced AI developers. Revenue growth remains important, but investors will increasingly assess operating costs, capital requirements and the path towards profitability.

Anthropic’s public listing could provide an important indication of how much investors are willing to pay for large-scale AI businesses. Strong demand could support confidence across the technology sector, while a weaker reception could renew concerns about elevated AI valuations.

For Nvidia and its technology partners, the central question is whether the enormous investment in AI infrastructure can translate into sustainable returns. Anthropic’s IPO will put that question directly before public-market investors.

I really wasn't expecting Microsoft Paint to turn up in the cybersecurity headlines, but here we are. MS disclosed and patched a security vulnerability within the software on October 13, but it's worth checking your own Paint version to see if you've received the update.

Some users don't have automatic Microsoft Store updates enabled, especially those on work machines. As Paint is updated via the store, you may need to manually update to version 11.2609.401.0 to keep your machine secure.

To confuse things further, International Cyber Digest reports that the MS store was providing older versions to some users until recently, and Windows Latest didn't seem to receive the new version even with auto updates enabled. Or, there's been some mixup with release numbers. It's genuinely difficult to tell at this point, with differing information coming from multiple sources.

The vulnerability was initially given an 8.1 severity rating on the CVSS scale, but Microsoft downgraded it to a 7.0 score on November 9 according to Threat Defence. MS also changed its description of the bug to that of a local exploit (requiring a user to open a crafted file) rather than a network attack vector, .

Regardless, to check whether your Paint version is hopefully up-to-date (what a world we live in), open the app, go to the Settings menu, and check the version number beneath the "About Paint" section. If it's lower than version 11.2609.401.0, open the Microsoft Store and manually update the app. Or, if your Windows 11 settings don't allow user updates, contact your sysadmin.

You can also check your Paint version within Windows Powershell by running:

Honestly, this is the biggest omnishambles of conflicting cybersecurity info I've seen in a while. Some outlets initially reported the bug as a no-click security flaw, while others have suggested that the vulnerability could potentially be exploited via a specially crafted image. Microsoft shifting the security goalposts as it goes really hasn't helped, nor has its apparently messy roll out of the patched version.

Police Raid $4.4 Million San Francisco Squatter Mansion—Arresting Woman and Rescuing 7 Dogs

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The woman allegedly at the center of a squatting dispute in San Francisco’s luxury Sea Cliff community was arrested on Friday during a raid of the property and following a monthslong investigation into her claims on the home. Diana Peng, 42, was charged with fraud, driving without a license, narcotics possession, improper care of animals, and being a felon in possession of a firearm and ammunition. Police also removed seven dogs from the home and put them in the care of a local shelter.

Peng and her alleged accomplice, Brandon Aadee, are in the midst of a civil lawsuit brought by San Francisco City Attorney David Chiu. Chiu accused the pair of illegally obtaining the $4.4 million property through forged, fraudulently notarized deeds, intending to sell it.

According to Chiu’s complaint, Peng and Aadee took over the 3,000-square-foot Tudor-style home at 250 32nd Ave. in 2025 by claiming that the previous owner had transferred the deed to Aadee. Peng and Aadee transferred the property title back and forth between them eight times, and at least five of those were forged, including the initial deed from the legitimate owner, Wei Wang, which includes a forged notary signature.

Wang and her husband, Xiangdong Guo, purchased the property in 2013 for $2.41 million. But the pair moved abroad around 2019, and the property fell into disrepair and appeared to be abandoned.

It also racked up more than $340,000 in unpaid property taxes, which prevented a sale of the property.

This past October, however, the San Francisco Board of Supervisors approved a resolution to pay off the property taxes, paving the way for the home to be put up for auction later this year.

Neighbors in the wealthy enclave, where both Sharon Stone and Twitter founder Jack Dorsey have homes, said they spent much of the past year on edge, watching people come and go from the property at all hours.

"I'm probably in touch with one city official or another at least six times every day," neighbor Whitney Lynn told ABC 7. "We really have been asking for help, and it feels like people finally heard us.Most recently, in August, a neighbor called 311 to complain that the property was “being used as a group home.”

“The home was abandoned for over 5 years, and squatters moved in May 2025 using burglary tools. Over the past couple of days, a new group of about a dozen squatters moved in," the complaint read. Nearly a dozen complaints were also filed with the Department of Building Inspection, the most recent on Oct. 13. That complaint stated that unpermitted plumbing and irrigation work had been taking place in the backyard.

Neighbors told the San Francisco Chronicle that Friday’s raid lasted about four hours and that police removed several boxes and bags of documents. Police went in with guns drawn and came out with Peng, and seven dogs were removed from the property and taken to a local shelter. Police also towed Peng's Porsche.

Deb Campbell, the public information officer with San Francisco Animal Care and Control, tells Realtor.com® the two adult pit bulls and five puppies are “doing well in our care.” She otherwise couldn’t comment on the animals' condition because of the ongoing investigation.

“We appreciate SFPD’s work to pursue accountability and keep our neighborhoods safe,” Jen Kwart, the communications director for the city attorney’s office, tells Realtor.com. "As our civil case moves forward, we look forward to continuing to work closely with SFPD to ensure the courts understand that Defendants are effectively trying to steal a house and sell it to make millions in profit.”

Both Peng and Aadee have long criminal records. In 2008, she was convicted of perjury and sentenced to 90 days in jail and three years’ probation. Four years later, she pleaded guilty to identity theft and burglary and served seven months in prison. And then in 2015, she was sentenced to an additional two years in jail for forgery of a driver’s license and using another person's identification.

In 2009, Aadee was convicted of defrauding FEMA out of thousands of dollars and ordered to pay $48,000 in restitution. He also has convictions for credit card fraud and passing bad checks.

Following Friday’s raid, Peng was booked and is being held without bail at the San Francisco County Jail.

Julie Gerstein is a writer and editor based in Philadelphia. She previously worked at Business Insider as an executive editor covering news, entertainment, and digital culture, and was the company's founding Singapore bureau chief. She was also a senior editor at BuzzFeed, covering lifestyle, fashion, and beauty. She earned a B.A. in women's studies from the University of Pennsylvania and an M.A. in media and cultural studies from the University of Sussex.

Il futuro di Neymar appare sempre più incerto. La stella brasiliana ha iniziato a valutare seriamente l'ipotesi di chiudere anticipatamente la propria carriera a causa del continuo calvario fisico e dei ricorrenti infortuni che ne limitano il rendimento. Secondo quanto riportato dal portale francese Foot Mercato, l'attaccante 34enne in forza al Santos ha confessato queste difficoltà al creatore di contenuti Cris Guedes a margine del torneo della Kings League, lasciando intendere la possibilità di un addio al calcio giocato prima di quanto ipotizzato in passato.

Nel corso della conversazione, dopo aver espresso l'intenzione di far parte della Kings League e di voler proseguire fino al 2030, Neymar è stato stuzzicato da Guedes circa la possibilità di spingersi oltre, dato che a quella data avrebbe compiuto 39 anni. Di fronte all'insistenza, il maggior marcatore della storia della Nazionale brasiliana ha frenato bruscamente le aspettative spiegando l'impossibilità di proseguire oltre: "Se avessi le mie ginocchia, capiresti".

AMD's Epyc X-chips are back after a three-year hiatus

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Venice-X promises big speedup for EDA and HPC workloads

AMD's cache-packed Epyc X chips are officially making a comeback.

Based on the House of Zen's frequency-optimized SKUs we looked at back in September, its HPC-optimized Venice-X parts will offer up to 96 cores and use the company's 3D V-Cache chip stack tech to triple the L3 cache density to more than a gigabyte.

But before you get too excited about another cache-packed Epyc, it'll be a little while before they actually ship. AMD expects the Venice-X to begin trickling out in the second half of 2027.

First teased at CES earlier this year, the chips are primarily aimed at latency-sensitive technical and scientific computing workloads like computational fluid dynamics and electronic design automation (EDA).

AMD itself relies heavily on its X-series Epycs for EDA workloads like functional verification testing, which is used to simulate silicon in order to ensure it behaves as it's intended to. According to AMD Fellow Phil Steinke, by keeping more of the model in cache, the CPU cores don't have to wait as long for data to move in and out of system memory.

Normally, boosting L3 capacity would have required larger chiplets with lower yields, but that's not how AMD has gone about it for the past four years. Instead, it used advanced packaging to give its existing CPU dies a little SRAM backpack.

This tech has been around for several generations now, having first debuted in the Ryzen 7 5800X3D back in 2022. That consumer desktop chip stacked a 64 MB SRAM tile on top of an 8-core chiplet, boosting L3 capacity to 96 MB. The extra cache made the chip one of the most potent gaming CPUs of the time.

Later that year, AMD brought the same technology to its Milan generation of Epycs as the company expanded its portfolio to address a wider range of workloads. The extra cache was popular enough to justify a second generation of X-series Epycs, which launched in 2023.

But then, nothing. When AMD unveiled its 5th-gen Epycs in late 2024, there was no Turin-X on the roadmap.

16 channels of DDR5, 5 GHz clock speeds, and support for CXL 3.1 memory god boxes? What's not to like?

According to Madhu Rangarajan, who runs AMD's compute and enterprise AI products biz, this was intentional. "We are not doing the X every generation.... The enterprise HPC kind of market tends to have a cadence that's a little bit slower, so that's what drives our cadence."

Venice-X marks the return of AMD's X-chips, and for the most part, the processor largely follows the same recipe as its predecessors. Each of the chip's up to eight CPU dies is now bonded to a 96 MB SRAM tile using advanced packaging, for a total of 144 MB of L3 per chiplet or 1,152 MB per socket, which you might have noticed is the same as in the Genoa-X parts they replace.

But while core count and cache capacity haven't increased, clock speeds certainly have, with Venice now hitting 5.3 GHz compared to Genoa-X at just 3.7 GHz.

"In Milan and Genoa, there was one trade-off. You either had to choose something that was high frequency or you chose something with 3D V-Cache." HPC likes both, Rangarajan told El Reg.

The method for integrating the SRAM tile required this kind of compromise. Stacking SRAM on top of the compute chiplets boosted L3 caches, but also trapped heat, which limited boost clocks.

So, for the Zen 5 generation, AMD moved the SRAM tile from the top of the stack to the bottom. With the SRAM tile no longer acting as a blanket, the chips could run hotter and faster for longer. But while gamers enjoyed the higher clocks and bigger caches on AMD's Ryzen 9000 processors, only now with Venice-X are those improvements making their way into Epyc.

Because customers no longer have to choose between big caches and fast clocks, Rangarajan believes the chip may see wider adoption. "I think it's going to end up being much bigger in HPC than the previous generations where we had to make that tradeoff."

Curiously, if frequency weren't such a high priority, Venice-X may not have needed 3D stacked SRAM in the first place. As we reported earlier this year, AMD's top-specced Venice CPUs boast 256 cores and 1,024 MB of L3 cache.

AMD could have just fused off 160 of the cores and achieved a similar cache-per-core ratio. In fact, AMD did something like this for the Turin generation: For the Epyc 9175F, AMD essentially took a 128-core part and fused off 112 of the cores. What was left was a 16-core part with 512 MB of last-level cache.

"You could do that [with Venice], but then you don't get the frequency part of it. The Fmax would be at 4.1 gigahertz and not 5.3 GHz," Rangarajan said.

"Venice X really changes the game," he added. "For the first time, you can get the larger L3 in our highest frequency part." ®

Harvard Bioscience (HBIO) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Academic weakness offset by China growth as a manufacturing overhaul nears.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Harvard Bioscience, Inc. (HBIO +2.20%) reported third quarter results that met management expectations, highlighting an 8% increase in revenue. Growth was driven by demand from contract research organizations (CROs) and performance in the China market, which offset ongoing weakness in the academic sector. The company is currently executing Project Viking, a manufacturing consolidation initiative intended to streamline operations and reduce annual costs starting in 2027. While revenue targets remain unchanged for the full year, management adjusted its profitability outlook to account for a product mix favoring lower-margin legacy items and higher distribution channel participation.

Building on the sales realignment led by our new Head of Commercial, we established a dedicated key accounts group, which is focused on managing and expanding relationships with our largest customers, specifically our top-tier pharmaceutical CRO and biotech customers.

Sales through Fisher Scientific North America have more than doubled year-to-date, following the expansion of our distribution agreement last year, reinforcing the strength of our diversified channel strategy. Turning to our operational initiatives. We continue to make steady progress on Project Viking. Our manufacturing consolidation plan which remains on target for completion in the first quarter of 2027. During the third quarter, we successfully completed the relocation of our amino acid analyzer product line to our Cambridge U.K. facility. We began shipping fluidics products out of our Minneapolis facility using a parallel build approach to ensure uninterrupted delivery to our customers.

As a reminder, we remain on track for Project Viking to generate $3 million in cost savings in 2027 and $4 million annually thereafter. These savings will contribute directly to adjusted EBITDA. Additionally, our newly formed Scientific Advisory Board held its inaugural meeting in August. We will hold quarterly meetings going forward. The input from this group of industry leaders will help guide our technology road map. Since its creation, the members of the Scientific Advisory Board have already provided me and our executive team with excellent industry insight and counsel. Turning to our outlook.

Based on our year-to-date performance and our commercial momentum, we are reaffirming our full year 2026 revenue guidance of 3% to 5% growth as well as our adjusted gross margin target of 57% to 59%. As noted in our earnings release, we've updated our full year adjusted EBITDA range. This adjustment directly reflects the effect from higher sales volume of lower margin legacy products and of shipments in China and through our distribution partners. While the product mix carries a lower gross margin, our operating expense remains disciplined, and we are guiding to EBITDA growth in Q4 at the midpoint of our outlook. In summary, Harvard Bioscience delivered solid top line performance in the third quarter.

Our core platforms are performing well. Our commercial initiatives are driving volume and our operational milestones under Project liking remained firmly on schedule. We are confident in the trajectory of the business and our positioning for 2027. With that, I will turn the call over to Mark for a detailed review of our financials. Mark?

Mark Frost: Thank you, John. I will start my comments with our third quarter 2026 financial results, the details of which can be found starting on Slide 4 of the earnings presentation posted to our IR site. Slide 4, revenue of $22.2 million was up 8% year-over-year and towards the high end of our guidance range and the second quarter in a row of strong revenue growth. The growth was primarily attributable to continued demand from CROs and robust sales from our distribution partners. By product, we had a strong quarter in organoids, fluidics and respiratory devices.

We're encouraged by the strong demand for organoid-related products with our broader new product innovation NPI portfolio attributing over 11% of total revenue as we work to expand our NPI contribution. In addition, we continue to see growth in telemetry in our preclinical platform. Our adjusted gross margin of 57.1% was at the low end of our outlook range as we continue to see greater demand for some of our lower margin legacy products had a higher contribution from our distribution channel and saw continued strength in China, where we generate a lower margin.

As we stated last quarter, driving sales of our higher-margin NPI products is a priority, but we're not going to miss opportunities to sell any of our products as we drive scale and we expect to realize significant benefits once our manufacturing consolidation is complete in early 2027. As a reminder, we have provided an adjusted gross margin reconciliation in the release to show the impact of restructuring, which is in the other line. Now a point for our investors as we operate our business, assuming the impact of depreciation, amortization and stock compensation costs. In addition, as we expand our Made in China initiative, we expect to improve our margins in that market.

OpEx increased $0.8 million in the quarter. As we have mentioned, we restored salaries, merit and bonus potential this year, which is the primary reason for higher OpEx and reflects a normalized cost structure. This investment is offsetting our improvements from higher revenue, but we will see improved leverage in our returns in 2027. Operating loss was $1.2 million compared to income of $0.2 million in quarter 3 2025, adjusted operating income was $1.2 million, 5.3% operating margin, down from $1.5 million, 7.4% operating margin in Q3 last year. Adjusted operating income was lower primarily because of expenses being normalized.

Now adjusted EBITDA came in within our guidance range of $1.8 million, 7.9% EBITDA margin down from $2 million, 9.6% EBITDA margin in quarter 3, 2025. The lower EBITDA was directly attributable to mix from products channel in China as discussed earlier in relation to gross margin. Now moving to Slide 5 for results by geography. Geographically, Q3 revenues in the Americas were $11.3 million, up 9% year-over-year, driven by strong growth from our Fisher contract and CRO customers. In Europe, Q3 revenues were $6.3 million, down 5% year-over-year and 3% lower on a constant currency basis as the academic environment in Europe continues to be weak.

And in APAC, Q3 revenues were $4.6 million, up 29% year-over-year, primarily driven by distribution sales of respiratory, organoid and fluidic products. Within APAC, China revenues were $2.8 million, up 31% year-over-year. Our Made in China localization initiative also continues to progress nicely as we continue to work on rolling out additional localized product lines. Now on the academic front, while the funding environment has been slower to recover than anticipated, that softness has been more than offset by robust demand across our CRO and distribution channels. Pharma and Biotech revenue also continued to grow well through both our direct and distribution channels. Now I'll now move to Slide 6 to discuss further financial metrics.

GAAP diluted EPS in Q3 was negative $0.58 compared to negative $0.28 in Q3 2025. Q3 adjusted EPS was negative $0.06 compared to a negative $0.04 in Q3 2025. All per share numbers retroactively reflect the 1-for-10 reverse stock split completed in March. I've mentioned in the past, the differences between GAAP EPS and adjusted EPS are typically the impact of stock compensation, amortization and depreciation as well now or restructuring charges related to Project Viking. These differences between net loss and adjusted EBITDA are highlighted in the reconciliation tables on Slide 12 and 13 and are all noncash items, except Project Viking costs.

Our cash used in operations through 9 months was $0.6 million compared to cash generated of $6.8 million in the same period in 2025. This change was primarily driven by increased inventory to improve product lead times and support manufacturing prebuild requirements for Project Viking as well as project Viking restructuring charges and higher interest costs from our debt deal. We closed the quarter with $4.9 million in cash and cash equivalents. Net debt stood at $35.1 million, up roughly $7.6 million year-over-year. Total debt was $37.2 million, reduced by $2.8 million in unamortized deferred financing costs associated with our December 2025 debt refinance, which will be amortized over the life of the facility.

Noncash deferred financing amortization was $0.3 million in each quarter with noncash exit fee accruals running at $0.2 million per quarter. Now to provide some context on our debt structure, we face no near-term principal obligations as amortization does not commence until late December 2027 and the facility matures in December 2029. As we complete Project Viking in quarter 1 2027, the sunsetting transition costs alongside our expected $3 million in net savings will significantly improve our cash generation and balance sheet in 2027. And in addition, our debt deal provides Harvard Bioscience, the ability to refinance term loan to aid debt of $10 million with an asset-based revolver.

We have made progress and hope to announce new as pertaining to putting a revolver in place. The conversion will provide interest savings, a reduction of our term loan B exit fee and further flexibility to manage our working capital. I'll now move to Slide 8 to discuss our outlook for the close of the year. In the fourth quarter, we expect revenue between $23.7 million and $25.5 million at the midpoint of $24.6 million, this represents mid-single year-over-year revenue growth. Adjusted gross margin between 59% and 61% and adjusted EBITDA between $3.8 million and $4.2 million, which represents 5% growth at the midpoint. Now turning to the full year.

We are maintaining our full year revenue growth guidance of 3% to 5% and our full year adjusted gross margin target of 57% to 59%. We are lowering our full year adjusted EBITDA growth guidance from 6% to 10% to negative 1% to 4% to reflect the greater-than-expected demand of certain low margin -- lower margin legacy products, increased sales through our distribution channels into China customers. We're encouraged by our performance so far this year and confident in the trajectory of the business. We've seen a return to sales growth that we believe is sustainable. And once our manufacturing consolidation is complete, we'll see meaningful benefits to our bottom line and cash generation.

Demand for our legacy products is strong. Demand for our NPI portfolio is growing and will continue to operate the business with discipline. I want to thank all of our shareholders for their support and we look forward to updating you on our finish to the year. With that, I'll turn the call back to our operator to take questions.

John Duke: Yes, absolutely. Thanks, Paul. In terms of how BTX electroporation grew, we -- year-to-date, we are strong double digits. And as you know, much of it is due to both hardware as well as our consumables. And we're seeing growth really across the globe for those products.

Upgrade your storage the smart way while prices are high

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

GTA 6 preload is officially underway, and in just a few hours your PS5 or Xbox Series X will know all of Vice City's secrets (it just can't tell you them until November 19). With a whopping 212GB file size, the latest instalment in Rockstar's blockbuster series is its heaviest yet, and that could spell disaster for your existing game library.

If you managed to upgrade your PS5 SSD ahead of 2026's price inflation, I tip my hat. If not, you've got some difficult decisions to make around which games to keep on your system to make way for that hefty GTA 6 preload. It's tempting to bite the bullet, pay for the storage upgrade, and keep all your favorites close by. If your library consists of a mixture of PS5 and PS4 titles, though, I'd urge you to consider another option instead.

Even on sale, fan-favorite PS5 SSDs can still run you $399.99 (that's a Samsung 9100 Pro down from a whopping $679.99 at Newegg) in 2TB configurations. If you split your library between old and new classics, though, the best PS5 external hard drives aren't seeing the same kind of price inflations.

That's where something like the Seagate Portable 2TB external hard drive comes in. I've got my entire PS4 collection stowed away on this thing, ready to play whenever I like without taking up space on my main drive. Considering that only PS5 games can truly take advantage of those SSD speeds, there's no point in making room for older releases on either the internal drive or my 1TB SSD expansion.

The PS5 can run PS4 games directly from external hard drives like Seagate's, but it can only store PS5 games on them. That's not the end of the world though. Waited hours for that Call of Duty download and don't want to go through it again next time you feel the itch to play? It takes far less time to transfer your data between storage systems than it does to redownload and install everything all over again.

Right now, the average price for a 2TB SSD at Newegg (taken from the first ten results) sits at around $316. With the prices the way they are, I recommend anyone who needs to upgrade right now aims for a $250 - $300 price point for 2TB. That's a big emphasis on need, though. If there's any way to get around swapping out that drive to accommodate your GTA 6 preload, take it.

That means external hard drive prices are dramatically reduced by comparison, saving you roughly $200 on your storage upgrade. Waiting while your games transfer or keeping your PS4 library separate is a small price to pay for that kind of cash.

Polymarket US trading volume jumps 266% despite regulatory battles

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Trading volume on Polymarket’s US exchange surged from June to October, fueled by sports betting, even as the platform faces mounting regulatory scrutiny.

Trading on Polymarket’s US exchange more than tripled between June and October, up about 266% as the company faced state and federal scrutiny.

Monthly volume rose to $14.77 billion from $4.04 billion over that period, according to data from analytics platform TickerTracker, which counts trades at the contracts’ full payout value.

Polymarket told CNBC on Tuesday that its annualized revenue had topped $1.8 billion, up from more than $1 billion in June.

Combo contracts, which bundle multiple predictions into a single wager, accounted for $8.6 billion, or 58.2%, of October’s volume. Sports contracts were the next-largest category at $4.57 billion, or 31%.

The platform’s US arm has grown rapidly since the company widened access in May, as states challenge whether federally regulated prediction markets can offer sports contracts without state gambling licenses. Polymarket began admitting US traders to the separate exchange in late 2025, three years after a settlement with the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) led it to block US trading on its international platform.

Polymarket US trading volume by category in October 2026. Source: TickerTracker

The growth also follows a surge in sports-related trading during the FIFA World Cup, which ran from June 11 to July 19. Soccer contracts generated $1.81 billion in trading volume in June, up from $85.4 million in May, according to TickerTracker.

Political markets also saw increased activity surrounding the US midterm elections. Election-related contracts generated $108.2 million in trading volume on Polymarket’s US exchange during the first 10 days of November, compared with $16.6 million throughout September. Despite the increase, political markets accounted for just 2.2% of the platform’s November trading volume.

Meanwhile, Polymarket is pursuing a $1 billion funding round at a reported $21 billion post-money valuation. Investment firm 1789 Capital, where Donald Trump Jr. is a partner, is reportedly leading the round with an expected contribution of about $300 million.

Related: Binance, Polymarket seek Polygon hard fork to recover $6.8M in stolen crypto

Despite the figures, Polymarket continues to face regulatory scrutiny on several fronts. The CFTC was reportedly investigating Polymarket earlier this year, while US Senators John Curtis and Adam Schiff separately called on the regulator in June to examine allegations that the company paid social media influencers to promote fabricated trades.

Responding to the allegations, the company told Cointelegraph that it was “conducting a comprehensive audit of active promotional content to ensure it complies with our standards, as well as applicable regulatory and legal disclosure requirements.”

Polymarket is also among several prediction market operators facing legal challenges from US states. New York Attorney General Letitia James sued the company in September, alleging that its sports-related event contracts violated state gambling laws. The lawsuit is part of a broader wave of state actions against prediction market platforms, including Kalshi, over whether their contracts fall under federal commodities regulation or state gambling laws.

The CFTC has defended its authority to regulate prediction markets, pursuing legal action involving 10 states over efforts to enforce state gambling laws against the platforms.

The jurisdictional dispute could ultimately reach the US Supreme Court. In September, New Jersey officials petitioned the court to hear their case against Kalshi, seeking a ruling that could clarify which authorities have jurisdiction over sports-related prediction markets.

Andrei and Libby Castravet have spent years entertaining 90 Day Fiancé fans with their up-and-down relationship. However, it would seem those days are now over. After missing out on appearing on the 2026 TV schedule, the couple have announced that they're leaving the franchise. And, with that, they're not mincing words about the situation.

While Libby soft-launched the departure with some posts on Instagram, Andrei was a bit more explicit. He popped up in the comments and shared a goodbye in a way only he could. For better or worse, this departure is unapologetically filled with Andrei's signature attitude (via John Yates):

For the imbeciles that watched for 10 years and were not entertained for what I bring to the table, suck it. Go color your hair pink and go protest lol. The other majority who understands what I was doing, I appreciate youuuu. I said it during the summer time before they started this new HEA, I'm not doing trash TV anymore. They don't pay enough. The best decision ever, watch what happens next. New project coming!

This situation marks just the latest instance in which a high-profile couple to depart the 90 Day Fiancé series. And only months ago, Shekinah Güven also vowed she'd never work with 90 Day again. It sounds like Libby and Andrei, at the very least, may have something lined up, though they haven't dropped any clues as to what that might be.

I'm Shocked By How Much 90 Day Fiancé's Sarper Reportedly Made Doing Stand-Up Comedy

Libby and Andrei leaving 90 Day Fiancé comes a year after the unexpected passing of her father and fellow 90 Day star, Chuck Potthast. She wrote about dealing with grief following his passing, and perhaps shutting the door on this chapter is a way for the couple to move on.

Their time in 90 Day was not peaceful and created major rifts in their family. Libby became estranged from her brother, Charlie, as well as her sisters, as they took issue with Andrei's brash attitude and the way he fit into their way of life.

As for what comes next, I could imagine they're hoping to get involved in a show that plays to his skills as a real estate agent. HGTV still has plenty of shows with which they could make that transition, assuming there's a casting agent out there willing to give the couple a shot.

Still, if I had a nickel for every time a 90 Day Fiancé star said they were leaving to do bigger and better things, I'd have a ton of nickels. Ultimately, many of these people leave and end up struggling to find another series to appear on. Angela Deem may be the most notable case of this, having gone from one of the franchise's biggest stars to being absent from TV. She's recently hinted that her comeback is coming, but no concrete details have been shared on that front, as of this writing.

It's worth noting that other 90 Day Fiancé fans aren't buying the Andrei & Libby news and feel that the couple will eventually appear in a future season of The Last Resort. I'm skeptical of that, but I can add that, of all the deception we've seen from 90 Day stars over the years about their true plans, Libby and her family are pros at keeping fans in the dark. For now, we can only wait to see what happens.

Democratic Sweep Puts Crypto Bill On a Cleaner Track

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Home » Regulations » Democratic Sweep Puts Crypto Bill On a Cleaner Track

Regaining the majority at the Senate has shuffled the cards for crypto legislation: will Democrats handle it better?

Democrats’ control of both chambers of Congress has raised fresh questions about how soon Washington can deliver a lasting framework for the digital asset industry.

The stalled CLARITY Act and related market-structure legislation now sit before a Congress that is expected to reopen the terms, priorities, and timing of any overhaul.

The CLARITY Act was meant to settle a core industry question: which federal regulator oversees different parts of the crypto market. Its path was already uncertain.

The new congressional balance adds further negotiation over consumer protections, disclosure rules, clearer exchange oversight, and the split of authority between agencies.

Democrats Want to Rewrite the CLARITY Act and Crypto Could Pay the PriceDemocrats are reportedly preparing a major rewrite of the CLARITY Act after their midterm victory.The proposed changes could give the SEC greater oversight of crypto markets. The language, highlighted by… pic.twitter.com/DepyDC7V2J

Democratic majorities are likely to place a crystal-clear emphasis on investor safeguards and enforcement tools before endorsing a broad framework favored by many digital-asset firms.

Quick legislation is not ruled out, but any significant bill that advances could look different and move more slowly than earlier expectations.

Recently, Federal agencies have continued examining registration pathways and rules for crypto trading platforms even as Congress deliberates.

Crypto firms therefore face an evolving patchwork of agency actions while lawmakers work toward a more permanent statutory settlement.

Industry legal observers now see comprehensive market-structure rules crucial for the CLARITY Act potentially slipping into 2027.

Bitcoin’s recent rebound and renewed inflows into spot Bitcoin ETFs show that institutional interest has held up despite the political uncertainty.

Those ETF flows have offered the market some support, yet they do not resolve the regulatory questions still facing exchanges, token issuers, and decentralized-finance projects.

Market participants can already gain exposure through regulated products while much of the underlying industry waits for clearer operating rules.

A longer legislative process would likely preserve that divide, favoring firms able to absorb compliance costs and adapt to agency guidance.

Australia's Cheapest New Car Is This $13,000 MG Hatchback, And It's Far From A Stripper

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Chinese cars have been beating down on long established performance and luxury car brands for a while now, all while costing a fraction of the price. From jumping over potholes to setting top speed records to beating Land Cruisers in off-road tests and even having emergency water travel capabilities, there are some mighty impressive pieces of kit across the Chinese car industry. What I'm most interested in, though, are the ultra-cheap economy cars that sell for the price of a used shitbox, and that's exactly what the MG3 offers to Australian car buyers. It's a compact hatchback with the face of a plecostomus, which isn't especially exciting, but its $13,020 USD price sure is.

It's no stripper, either. That bargain price includes features like Apple CarPlay and Android Auto, steering wheel controls, air conditioning, a digital driver's display, an auto-hold function, automatic power window switches, and adaptive cruise control with lane centering. It even has a standard feature that the new Toyota RAV4 doesn't even offer on its most expensive trim level that happens to be four times the price of the MG3: padded front and rear armrests. OK, so the MG3's actual MSRP in Australia is $13,890.85 USD at current exchange rates, but the fact that a dealer was even willing to discount it further is remarkable, too.

As you can see from CarSauce's review, the MG3 is woefully slow. Its 1.5-liter four-cylinder engine sends just 109 horsepower and 105 pound-feet of torque to the front wheels via a continuously variable transmission, which results in a leisurely 0-to-62 mph time of 12.57 seconds. It ain't quick, but it gets the job done, and again, it's a $13,000 car. The last car sold in the United States that was near the $13,000 mark was the diminutive Chevrolet Spark, which ran the 0-to-60 sprint in 10.8 seconds in Car and Driver's testing, so the MG3 isn't too far off that pace. Plus, looking beyond straight-line speed (or lack thereof), the reviewer even says it rides great and handles respectably, which are not always the case with bargain-basement cars.

Sadly the MG3's weak engine doesn't equate to exceptional fuel efficiency, with the presenter's real-world economy equating to about 36 mpg on the highway and more like 26 mpg around town. Those aren't abysmal numbers, just perhaps not as good as one might hope from such a weak engine in a tiny car.

Beyond the lower-than-low base price, the MG3's value proposition really comes into focus when you take into account its 10-year manufacturer warranty. For those who say, "why spend $13,000 on the cheapest new car available when you can buy a bigger, better used car for the same price?" Consider the idea of spending $13,000 on a car with a 10-year-long warranty versus spending that same amount on a used car with no warranty. The new car comes with an entire decade of mechanical coverage so the buyer doesn't have to worry about unexpected repair costs for those 10 years, whereas the used car could end up requiring costly repairs within the first year of ownership.

There are some quality concerns noted in the video, including infotainment software glitches and some apparent inconsistencies in the operation of those fancy automatic windows, but considering the price and that stellar warranty, I'd say the pros outweigh the cons in this case. For those who view their cars as another appliance, or those who simply want the financial security of not worrying about repair costs for a whole decade, the MG3 must be a pretty tempting option. Provided you live in Australia, of course — or Mexico, Europe, the Middle East, or other parts of the world where it's pretty damn cheap, too.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang’s affair with alleged Chinese spy: a timeline

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia co-founder CEO Jensen Huang has never been as flashy or controversy-prone as AI leaders like OpenAI’s Sam Altman or SpaceXAI’s Elon Musk. Now, the 63-year-old industry kingpin has admitted that he cheated on his wife of 40 years in an affair that could have ramifications beyond Huang’s personal life and potentially impact national security.

United States officials believe that Huang’s likely romantic interest, Megaspeed executive Alice Huang, is a Chinese spy who has been feeding information back to Beijing. (Alice Huang, a Chinese citizen, is of no relation to Jensen Huang.)

Megaspeed, a Singapore-based AI infrastructure company, had reportedly purchased $4.8 billion in Nvidia chips over the last two years. The U.S. and Singapore have both previously investigated Megaspeed over suspicions that the firm was illegally sharing chips with China.

The U.S. and China are embroiled in a fierce technological arms race to build the most advanced AI infrastructure, which could shape each country’s economic future, military prowess, and capacity for innovation. Nvidia is valuable in this competition as one of the world’s largest makers of AI chips, which are essential for accelerating AI development. But as an American company, Nvidia faces government restrictions on the sale of its most powerful chips to Chinese entities.

As the controversy unfolds, we are organizing the most relevant events into a reverse-chronological timeline to keep you up to date.

The Nvidia CEO pulled out of upcoming speaking engagements at the ElevenLabs Summit and Anthropic CEO Forum.

Alice Huang and her family reportedly built a network of companies in China and Singapore to acquire Nvidia chips, including founding a Chinese company called Shanghai Century Litong. Founded in 2023, it became a Nvidia preferred cloud partner giving it priority access to chips, the Information reported. The Info reported that Shanghai Century Litong. facilitated buying $7.5 billion worth of Nvidia’s chips, including banned chips.

Nvidia’s head lawyer, Tim Teter, intervened in a 2025 US government probe surrounding the suspected smuggling of AI chips by Megaspeed International, Bloomberg reported. Teter contacted Commerce Under Secretary Jeffrey Kessler asking questions about whether the Trump administration played a role in detaining Alice Huang.

In early 2025, Alice Huang and a business partner sought China-based investors for an Asian data center, promising they had no problem in securing GPUs, the Financial Times reported.

On Sunday night, President Trump weighed in on Huang’s controversy and showed support for the Nvidia CEO.

“I have not spoken to him about it. I just know he’s a fine person, but you never know what’s going on,” Trump said.

When asked if Huang should remain chief of Nvidia, Trump said, “I think so. Oh yeah, sure.”

Jensen Huang wrote in a letter to Nvidia employees — which was also published as a press release — that he cheated on his wife.

“I owe my family and all of you an apology,” Huang told his company. “I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.”

Huang did not mention Alice Huang by name in the message, but linked his infidelity to “the national security concerns raised in news reports.”

“Nvidia’s Board of Directors has initiated an independent investigation. I support the investigation and will cooperate fully. I love our country and our company. I am committed to protecting Nvidia and supporting America’s national security. I am proud and grateful to be an American,” Huang wrote.

Nvidia shares slid over 5% the following day.

Three Republican congressmen — Chairman John Moolenaar of the House Select Committee on China, Chairman Brian Mast of the House Foreign Affairs Committee, and Chairman Rick Crawford of the House Permanent Select Committee on Intelligence — sent a request to Huang for a closed-door joint briefing with their three committees.

“Recent reports raise significant national security concerns regarding the [People’s Republic of China]’s access to sensitive U.S. technology and information,” the chairmen wrote. “The issue of PRC infiltration and access to cutting-edge U.S. technology is core to the investigative work of our three committees this Congress. It is vital that Congress determines the extent to which U.S. national security has been placed at risk.”

U.S. officials had been investigating Alice Huang and Megaspeed for more than a year when the New York Times broke the story in late October that the two executives also had a close relationship.

A Nvidia spokesperson denied that this relationship was something out of the ordinary.

“Jensen’s interactions with Ms. Huang are not unusual or unexpected. Meetings with customers are part of a CEO’s job; Jensen talks with thousands of customer executives every year,” Nvidia spokesperson John Rizzo said. He added that “if the U.S. government believes that any person is a threat to American industry, it would let us know.”

U.S. officials circulated a secret memo among policymakers which warned that Megaspeed’s Alice Huang, who had a relationship with Jensen Huang, was a spy for China’s Ministry of State Security. This memo was not public knowledge until it was later reported by the New York Times.

Secretary of State Marco Rubio supervised the drafting of the memo.

Around the same time, per the New York Times’ report, U.S. President Donald Trump was briefed about intelligence findings that possible Chinese spy Alice Huang had formed a close relationship to Jensen Huang. Trump did not act on this information.

The Nvidia CEO, an ally of Trump, has repeatedly advocated for the U.S. government to let Nvidia expand its business in China.

Megaspeed was confirmed to be under investigation by both the U.S. and Singaporean governments in October 2025. As a customer of Nvidia, Megaspeed was suspected of helping Chinese companies get around American embargoes on chip exports.

Megaspeed, a neocloud that used Nvidia chips, had been renting computing power to Chinese internet giant Alibaba and other Chinese companies, the Times reported. A Commerce Department official who had visited Megaspeed’s data center in Malaysia in 2024 had found a hoard of Nvidia equipment that was still packed in boxes and the concern was that this equipment was was being directly shipped somewhere else, like China, rather than installed and sold as a cloud service.

Jensen Huang had been spotted socializing with Megaspeed executive Alice Huang as early as 2024 in Taiwan, where the Nvidia CEO was born.

These investigations into Megaspeed highlighted concerns about the degree to which Nvidia is able to track its chip exports. At the time, a Nvidia spokesperson told press that the company did not have “any reason to believe products had been diverted.”

“NVIDIA visited multiple Megaspeed sites yet again earlier this week and confirmed what we previously observed — Megaspeed is running a small commercial cloud, like many other companies throughout the world, as allowed by U.S. export control rules,” the Nvidia spokesperson said at the time.

Meta and IMAX sign deal to put an IMAX theatre in Meta’s VR Glasses

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

IMAX’s app will put Meta VR Glasses wearers in a virtual theatre, with some films in its exclusive Expanded Aspect Ratios. The $1,299.99 glasses launch in spring 2027.

IMAX and Meta have signed an agreement to make Meta’s VR Glasses the first VR device with IMAX Enhanced certification. IMAX will also bring an updated app to the glasses when they launch in spring 2027, the companies said on Tuesday.

The app will put viewers in a virtual theatre to watch IMAX Enhanced films and shows. Some titles will play in IMAX’s exclusive Expanded Aspect Ratios. IMAX says the aim is to bring audiences closer to the filmmaker’s vision, with optimised picture and sound.

“Meta is taking a bold new step in immersive entertainment, and we believe our brand and technology is a powerful differentiator for new and emerging devices like the Meta VR Glasses,” said Rich Gelfond, chief executive of IMAX.

Sony, Hisense, TCL and LG already make IMAX-certified TVs and audio devices. IMAX Enhanced content streams on Disney+, and on Sony Pictures Core through Amazon Prime Video.

Meta first said the glasses would be IMAX Enhanced certified when it unveiled them at its Connect event in September. The agreement with IMAX makes that official and adds the IMAX app.

The glasses go on sale in spring 2027 for $1,299.99. They sit above the $299 Meta Glasses that Meta launched in June.

Adobe and Google have teamed up to connect Drive, Gmail and Acrobat

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Some Adobe tools will end up in Google Search's AI mode, as well.

Adobe held its Max conference in Miami today and the company announced an updated partnership with the search giant Google. The two entities are developing integrations across software products that actually sound pretty useful.

First up, Adobe Acrobat is receiving deeper connectivity with Google Drive and Gmail. This will create a "direct pathway" between the software products, meaning that there will be an "edit with Acrobat" option in file previews. Editing PDFs can be a bit finicky and this should streamline the process a bit.

We're expanding our partnership with @Adobe to integrate Adobe's creative and productivity tools directly into @GeminiApp, @GoogleCloud's Gemini Enterprise, @GoogleWorkspace, and AI Mode in Google Search, while bringing Google's frontier models into more Adobe apps.

— News from Google (@NewsFromGoogle) November 10, 2026

This will also go in the other direction. Google's AI models will be added to Acrobat, which should "help people edit PDFs conversationally, understand the information in their documents and create polished assets like presentations, resumes and reports faster."

To that end, the companies say that users will be able to access Acrobat directly from the chat window in Gemini. This is even coming to AI Mode in Google Search at some point in the future.

Finally, Adobe is planning on integrating its products with Google DeepMind, to allow for "creative editing experiences." That's Google's cutting-edge AI research laboratory. We don't exactly know how this integration would work, but Adobe boasts that third-parties are already "exploring customization of a Google image model for Photoshop that could enable near-real-time edits."

Beyond this partnership, Adobe's Max conference spent a fair bit of time on AI tools. The company is bringing its Firefly AI assistant to much of its Creative Cloud suite of products. Firefly will soon also be able to handle multi-step tasks, taking a page out of the agentic AI playbook.

It's introducing an AI assistant to some of its mobile apps. This starts with Premiere and is designed to help people make edits using natural language. Adobe is first making this available in a private preview before a broader rollout.
